People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

So it’s less the weight itself, I guess, and more that it feels really out of control.\n\nDoctor: When you say you're 'tearing through takeout and snacks' at night, do you feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely more than what most people would eat in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know what “most people” would eat exactly, but it feels like a lot for me. Like I’ll forget meals completely some days, then after midnight I’ll be tearing through takeout and snacks like I didn’t even plan to. It’s the back-and-forth that scared me, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after those late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean… sometimes I feel kind of annoyed with myself after, like, “why did I do that so late?” But then I’ll get distracted because I’m thinking about work the next morning or whether I left something in the car—my schedule’s been kind of messy. So… maybe a little bad about it sometimes, but I haven’t really sat there feeling guilty in a clear way, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sort of. It’s not like I’m sitting there thinking, “I can’t stop,” exactly, but once I start after midnight it just kind of keeps going—takeout, snacks, whatever’s around. And then the weird part is the next day I might forget to eat entirely because I’m busy with calls or driving around for work. That’s really what freaked me out enough to come in, honestly, just how all over the place it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are related to a fear of gaining weight or becoming fat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I don’t love feeling gross after eating too much, but I’m not sitting there worrying I’m gonna get fat or anything. It’s more like my routine gets weird and then suddenly I’m eating whatever’s there. Like last week I bought these chips for the house and somehow they were gone before I even—sorry, that’s probably not the point. But no, I haven’t noticed it being about weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that you tend to eat more or less at those late nights when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe stress has something to do with it, but honestly it’s just been all over the place. Like some days I’ll get busy and realize I basically skipped meals completely, and then other nights after midnight I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and whatever snacks are around. That’s the part that scared me enough to come in. It’s not really a neat pattern, at least not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you're eating at those late nights?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it feels kind of out of control, but not like in a dramatic way. It’s more that my eating’s just been really all over the place. Some days I’ll totally forget meals because I’m running around, and then other nights after midnight I’m suddenly going through takeout and snacks like… I don’t even know, like I’m catching up or something. That’s the part that freaked me out enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more at those late nights when you're feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: Maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s specifically because I’m upset. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just how all over the place it’s gotten—like some days I’ll completely forget to eat, and then other nights, after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. I’ll be sitting there with, like, containers everywhere and wondering how that even happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating at those late nights is usually triggered by feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s usually because I’m emotional. Like, maybe sometimes, but the bigger thing is it’s just been really inconsistent. Some days I’ll realize I forgot to eat basically all day, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. That’s what kind of scared me into coming in, because it feels all over the place.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. That’s the part that freaked me out enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more at those late nights when you're feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: Maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s specifically because I’m upset. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just how all over the place it’s gotten—like some days I’ll completely forget to eat, and then other nights, after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. I’ll be sitting there with, like, containers everywhere and wondering how that even happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating at those late nights is usually triggered by feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s usually because I’m emotional. So… maybe a little bad about it sometimes, but I haven’t really sat there feeling guilty in a clear way, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sort of. It’s not like I’m sitting there thinking, “I can’t stop,” exactly, but once I start after midnight it just kind of keeps going—takeout, snacks, whatever’s around. And then the weird part is the next day I might forget to eat entirely because I’m busy with calls or driving around for work. That’s really what freaked me out enough to come in, honestly, just how all over the place it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are related to a fear of gaining weight or becoming fat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I don’t love feeling gross after eating too much, but I’m not sitting there worrying I’m gonna get fat or anything. It’s more like my routine gets weird and then suddenly I’m eating whatever’s there. It’s more that my eating’s just been really all over the place. It feels… not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really tracked the number, honestly. My jeans maybe feel a little different some days, but the main thing is my eating’s been all over the place. Like I’ll get to the end of a workday and realize I basically skipped meals, and then other nights I’m up after midnight just destroying takeout and snacks. It kind of scared me because I’m usually more routine than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like, “oh no, I’m getting fat” or anything. It’s more that the way I’m eating is freaking me out. Some days I’ll just completely forget meals, like I’ll be running around at work and then realize I never ate, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through takeout and snacks. So it’s less the weight itself, I guess, and more that it feels really out of control.\n\nDoctor: When you say you're 'tearing through takeout and snacks' at night, do you feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely more than what most people would eat in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know what “most people” would eat exactly, but it feels like a lot for me. Like I’ll forget meals completely some days, then after midnight I’ll be tearing through takeout and snacks like I didn’t even plan to. It’s the back-and-forth that scared me, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after those late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean… sometimes I feel kind of annoyed with myself after, like, “why did I do that so late?” But then I’ll get distracted because I’m thinking about work the next morning or whether I left something in the car—my schedule’s been kind of messy. So… maybe a little bad about it sometimes, but I haven’t really sat there feeling guilty in a clear way, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sort of. It’s not like I’m sitting there thinking, “I can’t stop,” exactly, but once I start after midnight it just kind of keeps going—takeout, snacks, whatever’s around. And then the weird part is the next day I might forget to eat entirely because I’m busy with calls or driving around for work. That’s really what freaked me out enough to come in, honestly, just how all over the place it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are related to a fear of gaining weight or becoming fat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I don’t love feeling gross after eating too much, but I’m not sitting there worrying I’m gonna get fat or anything. It’s more like my routine gets weird and then suddenly I’m eating whatever’s there. Like last week I bought these chips for the house and somehow they were gone before I even—sorry, that’s probably not the point. But no, I haven’t noticed it being about weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that you tend to eat more or less at those late nights when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe stress has something to do with it, but honestly it’s just been all over the place. Like some days I’ll get busy and realize I basically skipped meals completely, and then other nights after midnight I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and whatever snacks are around. That’s the part that scared me enough to come in. It’s not really a neat pattern, at least not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you're eating at those late nights?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it feels kind of out of control, but not like in a dramatic way. It’s more that my eating’s just been really all over the place. Some days I’ll totally forget meals because I’m running around, and then other nights after midnight I’m suddenly going through takeout and snacks like… I don’t even know, like I’m catching up or something. That’s the part that freaked me out enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more at those late nights when you're feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: Maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s specifically because I’m upset. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just how all over the place it’s gotten—like some days I’ll completely forget to eat, and then other nights, after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. I’ll be sitting there with, like, containers everywhere and wondering how that even happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating at those late nights is usually triggered by feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s usually because I’m emotional. Like, maybe sometimes, but the bigger thing is it’s just been really inconsistent. Some days I’ll realize I forgot to eat basically all day, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. That’s what kind of scared me into coming in, because it feels all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight changes, like noticeable weight gain or loss, over the period when your eating pattern has been all over the place?\n\nPatient: I mean, I haven’t really been tracking the scale like that. Like I’ll forget meals completely during the day, then it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through takeout and snacks. It’s embarrassing, like there’s wrappers everywhere, and then I’m thinking about work the next morning and how I’ve gotta be normal on sales calls. So… noticeable weight change, I’m not totally sure. The pattern is what I noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in how your clothes fit over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I haven’t really been focused on my clothes so much. Like some days my waistband feels weird, but then I’m also wearing different stuff for work, and half my laundry’s piled up, so it’s hard to tell. The thing that’s really been freaking me out is just my eating being all over the place—some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through takeout and snacks.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant weight loss during the time your eating has been irregular?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it significant weight loss, exactly. I’m not really keeping track like that. It’s just… not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your eating pattern became irregular?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if I’ve lost a significant amount, honestly. I haven’t been checking my weight like that. The thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating’s been all over the place—like some days I’ll just forget meals completely, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. It just feels really out of control.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased noticeably since your eating habits became irregular?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not sure if my weight has actually gone down noticeably. It feels… not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really tracked the number, honestly. My jeans maybe feel a little different some days, but the main thing is my eating’s been all over the place. Like I’ll get to the end of a workday and realize I basically skipped meals, and then other nights I’m up after midnight just destroying takeout and snacks. It kind of scared me because I’m usually more routine than that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like, “oh no, I’m getting fat” or anything. It’s more that the way I’m eating is freaking me out. Some days I’ll just completely forget meals, like I’ll be running around at work and then realize I never ate, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through takeout and snacks. So it’s less the weight itself, I guess, and more that it feels really out of control.\n\nDoctor: When you say you're 'tearing through takeout and snacks' at night, do you feel like you're eating an amount of food that's definitely more than what most people would eat in a similar situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know what “most people” would eat exactly, but it feels like a lot for me. Like I’ll forget meals completely some days, then after midnight I’ll be tearing through takeout and snacks like I didn’t even plan to. It’s the back-and-forth that scared me, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after those late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it guilt, exactly. I mean… sometimes I feel kind of annoyed with myself after, like, “why did I do that so late?” But then I’ll get distracted because I’m thinking about work the next morning or whether I left something in the car—my schedule’s been kind of messy. So… maybe a little bad about it sometimes, but I haven’t really sat there feeling guilty in a clear way, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating during those late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sort of. It’s not like I’m sitting there thinking, “I can’t stop,” exactly, but once I start after midnight it just kind of keeps going—takeout, snacks, whatever’s around. And then the weird part is the next day I might forget to eat entirely because I’m busy with calls or driving around for work. That’s really what freaked me out enough to come in, honestly, just how all over the place it’s been.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating patterns are related to a fear of gaining weight or becoming fat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I don’t love feeling gross after eating too much, but I’m not sitting there worrying I’m gonna get fat or anything. It’s more like my routine gets weird and then suddenly I’m eating whatever’s there. Like last week I bought these chips for the house and somehow they were gone before I even—sorry, that’s probably not the point. But no, I haven’t noticed it being about weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice that you tend to eat more or less at those late nights when you're feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe stress has something to do with it, but honestly it’s just been all over the place. Like some days I’ll get busy and realize I basically skipped meals completely, and then other nights after midnight I’m suddenly tearing through takeout and whatever snacks are around. That’s the part that scared me enough to come in. It’s not really a neat pattern, at least not that I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control when you're eating at those late nights?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it feels kind of out of control, but not like in a dramatic way. It’s more that my eating’s just been really all over the place. Some days I’ll totally forget meals because I’m running around, and then other nights after midnight I’m suddenly going through takeout and snacks like… I don’t even know, like I’m catching up or something. That’s the part that freaked me out enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more at those late nights when you're feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: Maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s specifically because I’m upset. Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is just how all over the place it’s gotten—like some days I’ll completely forget to eat, and then other nights, after midnight, I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. I’ll be sitting there with, like, containers everywhere and wondering how that even happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating at those late nights is usually triggered by feeling upset or emotional?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say it’s usually because I’m emotional. Like, maybe sometimes, but the bigger thing is it’s just been really inconsistent. Some days I’ll realize I forgot to eat basically all day, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. That’s what kind of scared me into coming in, because it feels all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight changes, like noticeable weight gain or loss, over the period when your eating pattern has been all over the place?\n\nPatient: I mean, I haven’t really been tracking the scale like that. Maybe my clothes feel different some days, but honestly the thing that freaked me out more was the eating itself being all over the place. Like I’ll forget meals completely during the day, then it’s after midnight and I’m just tearing through takeout and snacks. It’s embarrassing, like there’s wrappers everywhere, and then I’m thinking about work the next morning and how I’ve gotta be normal on sales calls. So… noticeable weight change, I’m not totally sure. I’m not really keeping track like that. Honestly, what scared me into coming in is the eating being all over the place — like some days I just forget to eat completely, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. It’s just… not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your eating pattern became irregular?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if I’ve lost a significant amount, honestly. I haven’t been checking my weight like that. The thing that scared me into coming in is more that my eating’s been all over the place—like some days I’ll just forget meals completely, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks. It just feels really out of control.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your weight has decreased noticeably since your eating habits became irregular?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’m not sure if my weight has actually gone down noticeably. I haven’t really been checking. What I keep noticing is my eating is just totally off—like I’ll forget meals completely some days, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout and whatever snacks are around. That’s the part that scared me, more than the number on the scale.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight since your eating habits changed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about noticeable weight, like I haven’t really been tracking it or anything. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t really know about weight, honestly. I haven’t been checking. The part that freaked me out is more that my eating’s been all over the place—like I’ll totally forget meals some days, and then other nights I’m up after midnight tearing through takeout and snacks.
